# Excalidraw Data

## Text Elements
let n = 4 piles of bananans ^0692kyEW

where each has piles[n] bananas ^qEzVDvIV

if koko decides to eat 3 bananas and piles[n] has 2 it only eats 2 bananas ^GsiPmMi5

Return the minimum integer k such that she can eat all the bananas within h hours. ^1xdD1tEo

Input: piles = [3,6,7,11], h = 8
Output: 4 ^s6G0BNs2

h = 8 hours ^6LPjeHTJ

Q1. What is the minimum value k could ever be? What is the maximum value k ever needs to be? (This defines your search space)
Q2. Given a fixed k, how do you calculate hours needed for one pile? What about a pile smaller than k?
Q3. Once you can calculate total hours for any k — how would brute force find the minimum valid k? ^7YlsrdnP

 1. The min value of k is for which she can eat all bananas in h hours is 1 while max value can the largest number of bananas in the piles array. so our array is asceding order 1.....maxValue
2. if i say k = 1 is speed of eating and we have 3 bananas in the first pile then time it takes to eat pile would 3 / 1 = 3 hours
3. Well we can start at k = 1 and see if totol hours are <= hours we have been given otheriwse increase k by one until we fulfill condition. ^I3URWK00

function minEatingSpeed(piles, h):
    maxSpeed = Math.max(piles)
    for k = 1 to maxSpeed:
        totalHoursTaken = timeTaken(k, piles)
        if ( totalHoursTaken <= h ):
            return k

function timeTaken(rate, piles):
    time = 0
    for i = 0 to piles.length - 1:
        time += ceil(piles[i]/rate)
    return time ^30lnc6BY

outer loop runs at worst runs for O(M) times where M is maxSpeed while inner loop again runs for O(N) times whole piles array so O(MxN) time complexity
space is constant since only using variables ^6RPHLfqa

since we know the range and also that its ordered we can use binary search here 
As in last ceil problem we simialrly have to find smallest value of the large values then a given value which is h so we can follow similar pattern here too
 ^ZXlykNuX

var minEatingSpeed = function(piles, h) {
    let left = 1
    let right = Math.max(piles)
    while( left <= right ) {
        let mid = left + ((right - left)/2)
        let totalHoursTaken = timeTaken(mid, piles)
        if ( totalHoursTaken <= h ) {
            right = mid - 1
        }else {
            left = mid + 1
        }
    }
    
    return left 
};

function timeTaken(rate, piles){
    let time = 0
    for i = 0 to piles.length - 1{
        time += Math.ceil(piles[i]/rate)
    }
    return time
} ^pAYH4Ij1

/**
 * @param {number[]} piles
 * @param {number} h
 * @return {number}
 */
var minEatingSpeed = function(piles, h) {
    let left = 1
    let right = Math.max(...piles)
    console.log(left, right)
    while( left <= right ) {
        let mid = Math.floor(left + ((right - left)/2))
        let totalHoursTaken = timeTaken(mid, piles)
        if ( totalHoursTaken <= h ) {
            right = mid - 1
        }else {
            left = mid + 1
        }
    }
    return left 
};

function timeTaken(rate, piles){
    let time = 0
    for ( let i = 0; i < piles.length; i++) {
        time += Math.ceil(piles[i]/rate)
    }
    return time
} ^Eo40esNF

Javascript Code ^lLQewg9Q

Time Complexity: O(log m * n) where m is the maximum number of apples on a tree and n is the number of trees in the apples array. Our binary search runs O(log m) times, and each time it runs it takes n operations to check how long it takes to collect all the apples for that rate.

Space Complexity: O(1) We are using only a constant amount of space. ^UANAxB30
